<p><b>Montserrat</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">MONT</span>-sə-<span style="font-size:90%">RAT</span></i></a>) is a <a href="/wiki/British_Overseas_Territories" title="British Overseas Territories">British Overseas Territory</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a>. It is part of the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands" title="Leeward Islands">Leeward Islands</a>, the northern portion of the <a href="/wiki/Lesser_Antilles" title="Lesser Antilles">Lesser Antilles</a> chain of the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies" title="West Indies">West Indies</a>. Montserrat is about 16&#160;km (10&#160;mi) long and 11&#160;km (7&#160;mi) wide, with roughly 40&#160;km (25&#160;mi) of coastline.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> It is nicknamed "The Emerald Isle of the Caribbean" both for its resemblance to coastal <a href="/wiki/Ireland" title="Ireland">Ireland</a> and for the <a href="/wiki/Irish_diaspora" title="Irish diaspora">Irish ancestry</a> of many of its inhabitants.<sup id="cite_ref-irishtimes_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-irishtimes-9">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-irishtimes-video_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-irishtimes-video-10">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Montserrat is the only non-fully sovereign full member of the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">Caribbean Community</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_of_Eastern_Caribbean_States" title="Organisation of Eastern Caribbean States">Organisation of Eastern Caribbean States</a>.
</p><p>On 18 July 1995, the previously dormant <a href="/wiki/Soufri%C3%A8re_Hills_volcano" class="mw-redirect" title="Soufrière Hills volcano">Soufrière Hills volcano</a>, in the southern part of the island, became active. Eruptions destroyed Montserrat's <a href="/wiki/Georgian_era" title="Georgian era">Georgian era</a> capital city of <a href="/wiki/Plymouth,_Montserrat" title="Plymouth, Montserrat">Plymouth</a>. Between 1995 and 2000, two-thirds of the island's population was forced to flee, primarily to the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a>, leaving fewer than 1,200 people on the island in 1997 (rising to nearly 5,000 by 2016).<sup id="cite_ref-mvo.ms_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mvo.ms-11">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2016Pop_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2016Pop-12">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> The volcanic activity continues, mostly affecting the vicinity of Plymouth, including its docking facilities, and the eastern side of the island around the former <a href="/wiki/W._H._Bramble_Airport" title="W. H. Bramble Airport">W. H. Bramble Airport</a>, the remnants of which were buried by flows from volcanic activity on 11 February 2010.
</p><p>An <a href="/wiki/Exclusion_zone#Natural_disaster_exclusion_zones" title="Exclusion zone">exclusion zone</a>, encompassing the southern part of the island to as far north as parts of the Belham Valley, was imposed because of the size of the existing volcanic dome and the resulting potential for <a href="/wiki/Pyroclastics" class="mw-redirect" title="Pyroclastics">pyroclastic</a> activity. Visitors are generally not permitted entry into the exclusion zone, but a view of the destruction of Plymouth can be seen from the top of Garibaldi Hill in Isles Bay. Relatively quiet since early 2010, the volcano continues to be closely monitored by the <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_Volcano_Observatory" title="Montserrat Volcano Observatory">Montserrat Volcano Observatory</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2015, it was announced that planning would begin on a new town and port at <a href="/wiki/Little_Bay,_Montserrat" title="Little Bay, Montserrat">Little Bay</a> on the northwest coast of the island. While additional plans proceeded, the centre of government and businesses was moved to <a href="/wiki/Brades" title="Brades">Brades</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> After a number of delays, including <a href="/wiki/Tropical_cyclone" title="Tropical cyclone">Hurricanes</a> <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Irma" title="Hurricane Irma">Irma</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Maria" title="Hurricane Maria">Maria</a> in <a href="/wiki/Tropical_cyclones_in_2017" title="Tropical cyclones in 2017">2017</a><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic" title="COVID-19 pandemic">COVID-19 pandemic</a> beginning in early 2020,<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> in June 2022, ground was broken on the Little Bay Port Development Project, a £28 million project funded by the UK and the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Development_Bank" title="Caribbean Development Bank">Caribbean Development Bank</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In 1493, <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Columbus" title="Christopher Columbus">Christopher Columbus</a> named the island <i>Santa María de Montserrate</i>, after the <a href="/wiki/Virgin_of_Montserrat" title="Virgin of Montserrat">Virgin of Montserrat</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_de_Montserrat" class="mw-redirect" title="Santa Maria de Montserrat">Monastery of Montserrat</a>, on <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_(mountain)" title="Montserrat (mountain)">Montserrat mountain</a>, near <a href="/wiki/Barcelona" title="Barcelona">Barcelona</a> in <a href="/wiki/Catalonia" title="Catalonia">Catalonia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> "Montserrat" means "<a href="/wiki/Serration" title="Serration">serrated</a> mountain" in <a href="/wiki/Catalan_language" title="Catalan language">Catalan</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/British_West_Indies" title="British West Indies">British West Indies</a>, <a href="/wiki/British_Leeward_Islands" title="British Leeward Islands">British Leeward Islands</a>, and <a href="/wiki/West_Indies_Federation" title="West Indies Federation">West Indies Federation</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Little_Bay_from_above,_Montserrat.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Little_Bay_from_above%2C_Montserrat.JPG/220px-Little_Bay_from_above%2C_Montserrat.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Little_Bay_from_above%2C_Montserrat.JPG/330px-Little_Bay_from_above%2C_Montserrat.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Little_Bay_from_above%2C_Montserrat.JPG/440px-Little_Bay_from_above%2C_Montserrat.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2736" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Little_Bay_from_above,_Montserrat.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A view of half of the coastline of Little Bay, and a glimpse of Carrs Bay, taken from partway up the headland between Little Bay and Rendezvous Bay, 2012</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pre-colonial_era">Pre-colonial era</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Pre-colonial era">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Archaeological field work in 2012 in Montserrat's Centre Hills indicated there was an Archaic (pre-<a href="/wiki/Arawak_peoples" class="mw-redirect" title="Arawak peoples">Arawak</a>) occupation between 2000–500&#160;<a href="/wiki/Common_Era" title="Common Era">BCE</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Later coastal sites show the presence of the <a href="/wiki/Saladoid" title="Saladoid">Saladoid</a> culture (until 550&#160;CE).<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> The native Caribs are believed to have called the island <i>Alliouagana</i>, meaning 'Land of the Prickly Bush'.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2016, nine <a href="/wiki/Petroglyph" title="Petroglyph">petroglyphs</a> were discovered by local residents hiking in a wooded area near <a href="/w/index.php?title=Soldier_Ghaut&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Soldier Ghaut (page does not exist)">Soldier Ghaut</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-22">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:2_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Another was discovered in 2018 in the same area of the island.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> The carvings are believed to be 1000-1500 years old.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-22">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_European_period">Early European period</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Early European period">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In November 1493, Christopher Columbus passed Montserrat in his <a href="/wiki/Voyages_of_Christopher_Columbus#Caribbean_exploration" title="Voyages of Christopher Columbus">second voyage</a>, after being told that the island was unoccupied due to raids by the <a href="/wiki/Island_Caribs" class="mw-redirect" title="Island Caribs">Caribs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A number of <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Ireland" title="Kingdom of Ireland">Irishmen</a> <a href="/wiki/Irish_immigration_to_Montserrat" title="Irish immigration to Montserrat">settled in Monsterrat</a> in 1632.<sup id="cite_ref-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._855_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._855-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Most came from nearby <a href="/wiki/Saint_Kitts" title="Saint Kitts">Saint Kitts</a> at the instigation of the island's governor <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Warner_(explorer)" title="Thomas Warner (explorer)">Thomas Warner</a>, with more settlers arriving later from <a href="/wiki/Virginia" title="Virginia">Virginia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_21-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The first settlers "appear to have been cultivators, each working his own little farm".<sup id="cite_ref-The_Island_of_Montserrat_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Island_of_Montserrat-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The preponderance of Irish in the first wave of European settlers led a leading legal scholar to remark that a "nice question" is whether the original settlers took with them the law of the Kingdom of Ireland insofar as it differed from the law of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_England" title="Kingdom of England">Kingdom of England</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Irish being historical allies of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_France" title="Kingdom of France">French</a>, especially in their dislike of the English, invited the French to claim the island in 1666, although no troops were sent by France to maintain control.<sup id="cite_ref-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._855_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._855-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> However, the French did attack and briefly occupy the island in the late 1660s;<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> it was captured shortly afterwards by the English and English control of the island was confirmed under the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Breda_(1667)" title="Treaty of Breda (1667)">Treaty of Breda</a> the following year.<sup id="cite_ref-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._855_25-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._855-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Despite the seizing by force of the island by the English, the island's legal status is that of a "colony acquired by settlement".<sup id="cite_ref-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._855_25-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Colonial_Law&#39;_1966._P._855-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/Neofeudalism" class="mw-redirect" title="Neofeudalism">neo-feudal</a> colony developed amongst the so-called "<a href="/wiki/Redlegs" class="mw-redirect" title="Redlegs">redlegs</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The colonists began to transport <a href="/wiki/Black_people" title="Black people">Sub-Saharan African</a> slaves for labour, as was common to most <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> islands.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_21-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The colonists built an economy based on the production of sugar, <a href="/wiki/Rum" title="Rum">rum</a>, <a href="/wiki/Arrowroot" title="Arrowroot">arrowroot</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sea_island_cotton" class="mw-redirect" title="Sea island cotton">sea island cotton</a>, cultivated on large plantations by slave labour. By the late 18th century, numerous <a href="/wiki/Plantations" class="mw-redirect" title="Plantations">plantations</a> had been developed on the island.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="18th_century">18th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: 18th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>There was a brief French attack on Montserrat in 1712.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> On 17 March 1768, a <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_slave_rebellion_of_1768" title="Montserrat slave rebellion of 1768">slave rebellion</a> failed but their efforts were remembered.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto1_28-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Slavery was abolished in 1834. In 1985, the people of Montserrat made <a href="/wiki/St_Patrick%27s_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="St Patrick&#39;s Day">St Patrick's Day</a> a ten-day public holiday to commemorate the uprising.<sup id="cite_ref-auto2_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto2-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Festivities celebrate the culture and history of Montserrat in song, dance, food and traditional costumes.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1782, during the <a href="/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War" title="American Revolutionary War">American Revolutionary War</a>, as America's first ally, France <a href="/wiki/French_capture_of_Montserrat" class="mw-redirect" title="French capture of Montserrat">captured</a> Montserrat in their war of support of the Americans.<sup id="cite_ref-auto2_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto2-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto1_28-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> The French, not intent on truly colonising the island, then agreed to return the island to <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Great_Britain" title="Kingdom of Great Britain">Great Britain</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_(1783)" title="Treaty of Paris (1783)">1783 Treaty of Paris</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="New_crops_and_politics">New crops and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: New crops and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Britain <a href="/wiki/Slavery_Abolition_Act_1833" title="Slavery Abolition Act 1833">abolished slavery</a> in Montserrat and its other territories effective August 1834.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto2_31-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto2-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto1_28-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the nineteenth century, falling sugar prices had an adverse effect on the island's economy, as <a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a> and other nations competed in the trade.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The first lime tree orchards on the island were planted in 1852 by a local planter Mr Burke.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Later, in 1857, the British philanthropist <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Sturge" title="Joseph Sturge">Joseph Sturge</a> bought a sugar estate to prove it was economically viable to employ paid labour rather than slaves.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_21-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Numerous members of the Sturge family bought additional land. In 1869, the family established the Montserrat Company Limited and planted <a href="/wiki/Key_lime" title="Key lime">Key lime</a> trees, started the commercial production of lime juice, with more than 100,000 gallons produced annually by 1895, set up a school, and sold parcels of land to the inhabitants of the island. The pure lime juice was transported in casks to England where it was clarified and bottled by Evans, Sons &amp; Co, of Liverpool, with a trade mark on each bottle intended to guarantee quality to the public.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Island_of_Montserrat_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Island_of_Montserrat-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_lime_industry_transport.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Montserrat_lime_industry_transport.jpg/220px-Montserrat_lime_industry_transport.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="172" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Montserrat_lime_industry_transport.jpg/330px-Montserrat_lime_industry_transport.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Montserrat_lime_industry_transport.jpg/440px-Montserrat_lime_industry_transport.jpg 2x" data-file-width="547" data-file-height="428" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_lime_industry_transport.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Barquentine" title="Barquentine">Barquentine</a> 'Hilda' loading lime juice<sup id="cite_ref-The_Island_of_Montserrat_26-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Island_of_Montserrat-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>Much of Montserrat came to be owned by <a href="/wiki/Smallholding" title="Smallholding">smallholders</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Connection_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Connection-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From 1871 to 1958, Montserrat was administered as part of the federal <a href="/wiki/Crown_colony" title="Crown colony">crown colony</a> of the <a href="/wiki/British_Leeward_Islands" title="British Leeward Islands">British Leeward Islands</a>, becoming a province of the short-lived <a href="/wiki/West_Indies_Federation" title="West Indies Federation">West Indies Federation</a> from 1958 to 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto_21-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The first <a href="/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Montserrat" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Minister of Montserrat">Chief Minister of Montserrat</a> was <a href="/wiki/William_Henry_Bramble" title="William Henry Bramble">William Henry Bramble</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_Labour_Party" title="Montserrat Labour Party">Montserrat Labour Party</a> from 1960 to 1970; he worked to promote labour rights and boost tourism to the island, and Montserrat's original airport was named in his honour.<sup id="cite_ref-source2_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-source2-41">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> However, Bramble's son <a href="/wiki/Percival_Austin_Bramble" title="Percival Austin Bramble">Percival Austin Bramble</a> was critical of the way tourist facilities were being constructed, and he subsequently set up his own party (the <a href="/wiki/Progressive_Democratic_Party_(Montserrat)" title="Progressive Democratic Party (Montserrat)">Progressive Democratic Party</a>) which went on to win the <a href="/wiki/1970_Montserratian_general_election" title="1970 Montserratian general election">1970 Montserratian general election</a>, with Percival Bramble serving as Chief Minister from 1970 to 1978.<sup id="cite_ref-RA_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RA-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> The period 1978 to 1991 was dominated politically by Chief Minister <a href="/wiki/John_Osborne_(Montserrat_politician)" title="John Osborne (Montserrat politician)">John Osborne</a> and his <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Liberation_Movement_(Montserrat)" title="People&#39;s Liberation Movement (Montserrat)">People's Liberation Movement</a>; his brief flirtation with possibly declaring independence never materialised.
</p><p>On 10 May 1991, the Caribbean Territories order came into force, formally <a href="/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_the_United_Kingdom#Abolition" title="Capital punishment in the United Kingdom">abolishing</a> the <a href="/wiki/Capital_punishment" title="Capital punishment">death penalty</a> for murder on Montserrat.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Corruption allegations within the PLM party resulted in the collapse of the Osborne government in 1991, with <a href="/wiki/Reuben_Meade" title="Reuben Meade">Reuben Meade</a> becoming the new chief minister.<sup id="cite_ref-SA_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SA-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> As a result, early elections were called.<sup id="cite_ref-SA_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SA-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1995, Montserrat was devastated by the catastrophic volcanic eruptions of the Soufrière Hills, which destroyed the capital city of <a href="/wiki/Plymouth,_Montserrat" title="Plymouth, Montserrat">Plymouth</a>, and necessitated the evacuation of a large part of the island. Many Montserratians emigrated abroad, mainly to the United Kingdom, though in recent years some have started returning. The eruptions rendered the entire southern half of the island uninhabitable, and it is currently designated an Exclusion Zone with restricted access.
</p><p>Criticism of the Montserratian government's response to the disaster led to the resignation of Chief Minister <a href="/wiki/Bertrand_Osborne" title="Bertrand Osborne">Bertrand Osborne</a> in 1997, after only a year in office, and being replaced by <a href="/wiki/David_Brandt_(politician)" title="David Brandt (politician)">David Brandt</a> who remained in office until 2001. Since leaving office, Brandt has been the subject of multiple criminal investigation into alleged sex offences with minors.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> He was found guilty of six counts of sexual exploitation and sentenced to fifteen years in July 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>John Osborne returned as Chief Minister following victory in the 2001 election, being ousted by <a href="/wiki/Lowell_Lewis" title="Lowell Lewis">Lowell Lewis</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_Democratic_Party" title="Montserrat Democratic Party">Montserrat Democratic Party</a> in 2006. Reuben Meade returned to office in 2009 to 2014;<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> during his term the post of Chief Minister was replaced with that of <a href="/wiki/Premier_of_Montserrat" title="Premier of Montserrat">Premier</a>.
</p><p>In the autumn of 2017, Montserrat was not hit by <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Irma" title="Hurricane Irma">Hurricane Irma</a> and sustained only minor damage from <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Maria" title="Hurricane Maria">Hurricane Maria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since November 2019, <a href="/wiki/Easton_Taylor-Farrell" title="Easton Taylor-Farrell">Easton Taylor-Farrell</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Movement_for_Change_and_Prosperity" title="Movement for Change and Prosperity">Movement for Change and Prosperity</a> party has been the island's Premier.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Politics and government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Montserrat" title="Politics of Montserrat">Politics of Montserrat</a></div>
<p>Montserrat is an internally self-governing <a href="/wiki/British_overseas_territory" class="mw-redirect" title="British overseas territory">overseas territory</a> of the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> Committee on Decolonization includes Montserrat on the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories" title="United Nations list of non-self-governing territories">United Nations list of non-self-governing territories</a>. The island's <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> is <a href="/wiki/King_Charles_III" class="mw-redirect" title="King Charles III">King Charles III</a>, represented by an appointed Governor. <a href="/wiki/Executive_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Executive power">Executive power</a> is exercised by the government, whereas the Premier is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>. The Premier is appointed by the Governor from among the members of the Legislative Assembly which consists of nine elected members. The leader of the party with a majority of seats is usually the one who is appointed.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Legislative_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative power">Legislative power</a> is vested in both the <a href="/wiki/Government" title="Government">government</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_Montserrat" title="Legislative Assembly of Montserrat">Legislative Assembly</a>. The Assembly also includes two <i><a href="/wiki/Ex_officio" class="mw-redirect" title="Ex officio">ex officio</a></i> members, the <a href="/wiki/Attorney_general" title="Attorney general">attorney general</a> and financial secretary.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Judiciary" title="Judiciary">Judiciary</a> is independent of the executive and the legislature.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_Divisions">Administrative Divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Administrative Divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:132px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Parishes_of_Montserrat.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Parishes_of_Montserrat.svg/130px-Parishes_of_Montserrat.svg.png" decoding="async" width="130" height="197" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Parishes_of_Montserrat.svg/195px-Parishes_of_Montserrat.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Parishes_of_Montserrat.svg/260px-Parishes_of_Montserrat.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="264" data-file-height="401" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Parishes_of_Montserrat.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><b>Parishes</b><br />St. Peter (red)<br />St. Georges (green)<br />St. Anthony (cyan)<br /><abbr title="abandoned and destroyed former capital city">Plymouth</abbr> (◾)</div></div></div><p>For the purposes of local government, Montserrat is divided into three <a href="/wiki/Parish_(administrative_division)" title="Parish (administrative division)">parishes</a>. Going north to south, they are:
</p><ul><li>Saint Peter Parish</li>
<li>Saint Georges Parish</li>
<li>Saint Anthony Parish</li></ul>
<p>The locations of settlements on the island have been vastly changed since the volcanic activity began. Only Saint Peter Parish in the northwest of the island is now inhabited, with a population of between 4000 and 6000,<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> the other two parishes being still too dangerous to inhabit.
</p><p><br />
A significantly more up-to-date administrative division type would be the 3 Census regions, primarily used for the population census.<a rel="nofollow" class="external autonumber" href="https://www.citypopulation.de/en/montserrat/">[2]</a> Going north to south, they are:
</p>
<ul><li>Northern Region (2,369 Pop)</li>
<li>Central Region (1,666 Pop)</li>
<li>South of Nantes river (887 Pop)</li></ul>
<p><br />
For census purposes, these are further divided into 23 Enumeration districts.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Police">Police</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Police">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Policing is primarily the responsibility of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Montserrat_Police_Service" title="Royal Montserrat Police Service">Royal Montserrat Police Service</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military_and_Defence">Military and Defence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Military and Defence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The defence of Montserrat is the responsibility of the United Kingdom. The <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a> maintains a ship on permanent station in the Caribbean (<a href="/wiki/HMS_Medway_(P223)" title="HMS Medway (P223)">HMS&#160;<i>Medway</i></a>)<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> and additionally sends another <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a> or <a href="/wiki/Royal_Fleet_Auxiliary" title="Royal Fleet Auxiliary">Royal Fleet Auxiliary</a> ship as a part of the Atlantic Patrol (NORTH) tasking. These ships' main mission in the region is to maintain British sovereignty for the overseas territories, provide humanitarian aid and disaster relief during disasters such as hurricanes, which are common in the area, and conduct counter-narcotics operations.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Royal_Montserrat_Defence_Force">Royal Montserrat Defence Force</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Royal Montserrat Defence Force">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Royal_Montserrat_Defence_Force" title="Royal Montserrat Defence Force">Royal Montserrat Defence Force</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Royal_Montserrat_Defence_Force" title="Royal Montserrat Defence Force">Royal Montserrat Defence Force</a> is the home defence unit of the British Overseas Territory of Montserrat. Raised in 1899, the unit is today a reduced force of about forty volunteer soldiers, primarily concerned with civil defence and ceremonial duties. The unit has a historical association with the Irish Guards.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Communications">Communications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Communications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The island is served by landline telephones, fully digitalised, with 3000 subscribers and by mobile cellular, with an estimated number of 5000 handsets in use. An estimated 2860 users have internet access. These are July 2016 estimates. Public radio service is provided by <a href="/wiki/Radio_Montserrat" title="Radio Montserrat">Radio Montserrat</a>. There is a single television broadcaster, PTV.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Cable and satellite television service is available.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_8-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Postal_codes_in_the_Pitcairn_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Postal codes in the Pitcairn Islands">UK Postcode</a> for directing mail to Montserrat is MSR followed by four digits according to the destination town, for example, the postcode for Little Bay is MSR1120.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Coastal_cliffs.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Coastal_cliffs.jpg/220px-Coastal_cliffs.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Coastal_cliffs.jpg/330px-Coastal_cliffs.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Coastal_cliffs.jpg/440px-Coastal_cliffs.jpg 2x" data-file-width="480" data-file-height="360" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Coastal_cliffs.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Montserrat's coastline</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Montserrat" title="Geography of Montserrat">Geography of Montserrat</a></div>
<p>The island of Montserrat is located approximately 25 miles (40&#160;km) south-west of <a href="/wiki/Antigua" title="Antigua">Antigua</a>, 13 miles (21&#160;km) south-east of <a href="/wiki/Redonda" title="Redonda">Redonda</a> (a small island owned by <a href="/wiki/Antigua_and_Barbuda" title="Antigua and Barbuda">Antigua and Barbuda</a>), and 35 miles (56&#160;km) north-west of the French overseas region of <a href="/wiki/Guadeloupe" title="Guadeloupe">Guadeloupe</a>. Beyond Redonda lies <a href="/wiki/Nevis" title="Nevis">Nevis</a> (part of <a href="/wiki/St_Kitts_and_Nevis" class="mw-redirect" title="St Kitts and Nevis">St Kitts and Nevis</a>), about 30 miles (48&#160;km) to the north-west. It comprises 104&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (40&#160;sq&#160;mi) and is gradually increasing owing to the buildup of volcanic deposits on the south-east coast. The island is 16&#160;km (9.9&#160;mi) long and 11&#160;km (6.8&#160;mi) wide and consists of a mountainous interior surrounded by a flatter littoral region, with rock cliffs rising 15 to 30&#160;m (49 to 98&#160;ft) above the sea and a number of smooth bottomed sandy beaches scattered among <a href="/wiki/Cove" title="Cove">coves</a> on the western (Caribbean Sea) side of the island. The major mountains are (from north to south) <a href="/w/index.php?title=Silver_Hill,_Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Silver Hill, Montserrat (page does not exist)">Silver Hill</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Katy_Hill,_Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Katy Hill, Montserrat (page does not exist)">Katy Hill</a> in the Centre Hills range, the <a href="/wiki/Soufri%C3%A8re_Hills" title="Soufrière Hills">Soufrière Hills</a> and the <a href="/w/index.php?title=South_Soufri%C3%A8re_Hills&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="South Soufrière Hills (page does not exist)">South Soufrière Hills</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto2_31-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto2-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The Soufrière Hills <a href="/wiki/Volcano" title="Volcano">volcano</a> is the island's highest point; its pre-1995 height was 915 metres (3,002&#160;ft), however it has now grown due post-eruption due to the creation of a <a href="/wiki/Lava_dome" title="Lava dome">lava dome</a>, with its current height being estimated at 1,050 metres (3,440&#160;ft).<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_8-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 2011 estimate by the CIA indicates that 30% of the island's land is classified as agricultural, 20% as arable, 25% as forest and the balance as "other".<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_8-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Montserrat has a few tiny off-shore islands, such as <a href="/w/index.php?title=Little_Redonda&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Little Redonda (page does not exist)">Little Redonda</a> off its north coast and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Pinnacle_Rock,_Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Pinnacle Rock, Montserrat (page does not exist)">Pinnacle Rock</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Statue_Rock&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Statue Rock (page does not exist)">Statue Rock</a> off its east.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Volcano_and_exclusion_zone">Volcano and exclusion zone</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Volcano and exclusion zone">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Salem_Eruption1.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Montserrat_Salem_Eruption1.jpg/220px-Montserrat_Salem_Eruption1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/eb/Montserrat_Salem_Eruption1.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="201" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Salem_Eruption1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Eruption of the Soufrière Hills volcano on 22 September 1997</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_eruption.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Montserrat_eruption.JPG/220px-Montserrat_eruption.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="138" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Montserrat_eruption.JPG/330px-Montserrat_eruption.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Montserrat_eruption.JPG/440px-Montserrat_eruption.JPG 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_eruption.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Plymouth,_Montserrat" title="Plymouth, Montserrat">Plymouth City</a> (former capital and major port of Montserrat) on 12 July 1997, after <a href="/wiki/Pyroclastic_flow" title="Pyroclastic flow">pyroclastic flows</a> burned much of what was not covered in ash</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Soufriere_Hills_Volcano_dome_collapse.gif" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Soufriere_Hills_Volcano_dome_collapse.gif/220px-Soufriere_Hills_Volcano_dome_collapse.gif" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Soufriere_Hills_Volcano_dome_collapse.gif/330px-Soufriere_Hills_Volcano_dome_collapse.gif 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Soufriere_Hills_Volcano_dome_collapse.gif/440px-Soufriere_Hills_Volcano_dome_collapse.gif 2x" data-file-width="1791" data-file-height="1791" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Soufriere_Hills_Volcano_dome_collapse.gif" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>False-colour time-lapse images of the Soufrière Hills volcanic dome collapse in 2010, from <a href="/wiki/NASA" title="NASA">NASA</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Plymouth_City_%26_Volcano.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Plymouth_City_%26_Volcano.jpg/170px-Plymouth_City_%26_Volcano.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Plymouth_City_%26_Volcano.jpg/255px-Plymouth_City_%26_Volcano.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Plymouth_City_%26_Volcano.jpg/340px-Plymouth_City_%26_Volcano.jpg 2x" data-file-width="768" data-file-height="1024" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Plymouth_City_%26_Volcano.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Devastated Plymouth City and volcano (2003)</div></div></div>
<p>In July 1995, Montserrat's Soufrière Hills volcano, dormant for centuries, erupted and soon buried the island's capital, Plymouth, in more than 12 metres (39&#160;ft) of mud, destroyed its airport and docking facilities, and rendered the southern part of the island, now termed the exclusion zone, uninhabitable and not safe for travel. The southern part of the island was evacuated and visits are severely restricted.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The exclusion zone also includes two sea areas adjacent to the land areas of most volcanic activity.<sup id="cite_ref-mvo.ms_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mvo.ms-11">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the destruction of Plymouth and disruption of the economy, more than half of the population left the island, which also lacked housing. During the late 1990s, additional eruptions occurred. On 25 June 1997, a pyroclastic flow travelled down Mosquito <a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/ghaut" class="extiw" title="wikt:ghaut">Ghaut</a>. This <a href="/wiki/Pyroclastic_surge" title="Pyroclastic surge">pyroclastic surge</a> could not be restrained by the ghaut and spilled out of it, killing 19 people who were in the (officially evacuated) Streatham village area. Several others in the area suffered severe burns.
</p><p>In recognition of the disaster, in 1998, the people of Montserrat were granted full residency rights in the United Kingdom, allowing them to migrate if they chose. British citizenship was granted in 2002.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For a number of years in the early 2000s, the volcano's activity consisted mostly of infrequent ventings of <a href="/wiki/Volcanic_ash" title="Volcanic ash">ash</a> into the uninhabited areas in the south. The ash falls occasionally extended into the northern and western parts of the island. In the most recent period of increased activity at the Soufrière Hills volcano, from November 2009 through February 2010, ash vented and there was a <a href="/wiki/Vulcanian_eruption" title="Vulcanian eruption">vulcanian explosion</a> that sent <a href="/wiki/Pyroclastic_flow" title="Pyroclastic flow">pyroclastic flows</a> down several sides of the mountain. Travel into parts of the exclusion zone was occasionally allowed, though only by a licence from the Royal Montserrat Police Force.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Since 2014 the area has been split into multiple subzones with varying entry and use restrictions, based on volcanic activity: some areas even being (in 2020) open 24 hours and inhabited. The most dangerous zone, which includes the former capital, remains forbidden to casual visitors due to volcanic and other hazards, especially due to the lack of maintenance in destroyed areas. It is legal to visit this area when accompanied by a government-authorized guide.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The northern part of Montserrat has largely been unaffected by volcanic activity, and remains lush and green. In February 2005, <a href="/wiki/Anne,_Princess_Royal" title="Anne, Princess Royal">Princess Anne</a> officially opened what is now called the <a href="/wiki/John_A._Osborne_Airport" title="John A. Osborne Airport">John A. Osborne Airport</a> in the north. Since 2011, it handles several flights daily operated by <a href="/wiki/FlyMontserrat" title="FlyMontserrat">Fly Montserrat Airways</a>. Docking facilities are in place at Little Bay, where the new capital town is being constructed; the new government centre is at Brades, a short distance away.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Wildlife">Wildlife</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Wildlife">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Montserrat" title="List of birds of Montserrat">List of birds of Montserrat</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_Montserrat" title="List of mammals of Montserrat">List of mammals of Montserrat</a>, and <a href="/wiki/List_of_amphibians_and_reptiles_of_Montserrat" title="List of amphibians and reptiles of Montserrat">List of amphibians and reptiles of Montserrat</a></div>
<p>Montserrat, like many isolated islands, is home to rare, <a href="/wiki/Endemic" class="mw-redirect" title="Endemic">endemic</a> plant and animal species. Work undertaken by the Montserrat National Trust in collaboration with the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Botanic_Gardens,_Kew" title="Royal Botanic Gardens, Kew">Royal Botanic Gardens, Kew</a> has centred on the conservation of pribby (<i>Rondeletia buxifolia</i>) in the Centre Hills region. Until 2006, this species was known only from one book about the vegetation of Montserrat.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> In 2006, conservationists also rescued several plants of the endangered Montserrat orchid (<i>Epidendrum montserratense</i>) from dead trees on the island and installed them in the security of the island's botanic garden.
</p><p>Montserrat is also home to the critically endangered <a href="/wiki/Leptodactylus_fallax" title="Leptodactylus fallax">giant ditch frog</a> (<i>Leptodactylus fallax</i>), known locally as the mountain chicken, found only in Montserrat and <a href="/wiki/Dominica" title="Dominica">Dominica</a>. The species has undergone catastrophic declines due to the amphibian disease <a href="/wiki/Chytridiomycosis" title="Chytridiomycosis">Chytridiomycosis</a> and the volcanic eruption in 1997. Experts from <a href="/wiki/Durrell_Wildlife_Conservation_Trust" title="Durrell Wildlife Conservation Trust">Durrell Wildlife Conservation Trust</a> have been working with the Montserrat Department of Environment to conserve the frog <a href="/wiki/In-situ_conservation" class="mw-redirect" title="In-situ conservation">in-situ</a> in a project called "Saving the Mountain Chicken",<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> and an <a href="/wiki/Ex-situ_conservation" class="mw-redirect" title="Ex-situ conservation">ex-situ</a> captive breeding population has been set up in partnership with Durrell Wildlife Conservation Trust, <a href="/wiki/Zoological_Society_of_London" title="Zoological Society of London">Zoological Society of London</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chester_Zoo" title="Chester Zoo">Chester Zoo</a>, Parken Zoo, and the Governments of Montserrat and Dominica. Releases from this programme have already taken place in a hope to increase the numbers of the frog and reduce extinction risk from Chytridiomycosis.
</p><p>The national bird is the endemic <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_oriole" title="Montserrat oriole">Montserrat oriole</a> (<i>Icterus oberi</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/IUCN_Red_List" title="IUCN Red List">IUCN Red List</a> classifies it as vulnerable, having previously listed it as critically endangered.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Captive populations are held in several zoos in the UK including: Chester Zoo, <a href="/wiki/London_Zoo" title="London Zoo">London Zoo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jersey_Zoo" title="Jersey Zoo">Jersey Zoo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Edinburgh_Zoo" title="Edinburgh Zoo">Edinburgh Zoo</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_galliwasp" title="Montserrat galliwasp">Montserrat galliwasp</a> (<i>Diploglossus montisserrati</i>), a type of lizard, is endemic to Montserrat and is listed on the IUCN Red List as critically endangered.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> A species action plan has been developed for this species.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2005, a biodiversity assessment for the Centre Hills was conducted. To support the work of local conservationists, a team of international partners, including Durrell Wildlife Conservation Trust, Royal Botanic Gardens, Kew, <a href="/wiki/Royal_Society_for_the_Protection_of_Birds" title="Royal Society for the Protection of Birds">Royal Society for the Protection of Birds</a> and <a href="/wiki/Montana_State_University" title="Montana State University">Montana State University</a>, carried out extensive surveys and collected biological data.<sup id="cite_ref-durrell.org_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-durrell.org-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Researchers from Montana State University found that the invertebrate fauna was particularly rich on the island. The report found that the number of invertebrate species known to occur in Montserrat is 1241. The number of known beetle species is 718 species from 63 families. It is estimated that 120 invertebrates are endemic to Montserrat.<sup id="cite_ref-durrell.org_68-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-durrell.org-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Montserrat is known for its coral reefs and its caves along the shore. These caves house many species of bats, and efforts are underway to monitor and protect the ten species of bats from extinction.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Montserrat tarantula (<i><a href="/wiki/Cyrtopholis" title="Cyrtopholis">Cyrtopholis</a> femoralis</i>) is the only species of tarantula native to the island. It was first bred in captivity at the Chester Zoo in August 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Montserrat has a <a href="/wiki/Tropical_rainforest_climate" title="Tropical rainforest climate">tropical rainforest climate</a> (<i>Af</i> according to the <a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification" title="Köppen climate classification">Köppen climate classification</a>) with the temperature being warm and consistent year-round, and lots of precipitation. Summer and autumn are wetter because of <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_hurricane" title="Atlantic hurricane">Atlantic hurricanes</a>.
</p>
