<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Caribe_Queen.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Caribe_Queen.jpg/220px-Caribe_Queen.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Caribe_Queen.jpg/330px-Caribe_Queen.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Caribe_Queen.jpg/440px-Caribe_Queen.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Caribe_Queen.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Motor_ship" title="Motor ship">MV</a> <i>Caribe Queen</i> is a <a href="/wiki/Nevis" title="Nevis">Nevis</a> ferry boat which shuttles passengers between <a href="/wiki/Antigua" title="Antigua">Antigua</a> and Montserrat several times a week</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Montserrat_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Montserrat_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Montserrat_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Montserrat_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Montserrat_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Montserrat_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Montserrat exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Montserrat" title="Economy of Montserrat">Economy of Montserrat</a></div>
<p>Montserrat's economy was devastated by the 1995 eruption and its aftermath;<sup id="cite_ref-auto2_31-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto2-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> currently the island's operating budget is largely supplied by the British government and administered through the <a href="/wiki/Department_for_International_Development" title="Department for International Development">Department for International Development</a> (DFID) amounting to approximately £25&#160;million per year. Additional amounts are secured through income and property taxes, licence and other fees as well as customs duties levied on imported goods.
</p><p>The limited economy of Montserrat, with a population under 5000, consumes 2.5 MW of electric power,<sup id="cite_ref-:0_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> produced by five diesel generators.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Two exploratory <a href="/wiki/Geothermal_power" title="Geothermal power">geothermal wells</a> have found good resources and the pad for a third geothermal well was prepared in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Together the geothermal wells are expected to produce more power than the island requires.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> A 250&#160;kW solar PV station was commissioned in 2019, with plans for another 750&#160;kW.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A report published by the CIA indicates that the value of exports totalled the equivalent of US$5.7&#160;million (2017 est.), consisting primarily of electronic components, plastic bags, apparel, hot peppers, limes, live plants and cattle. The value of imports totalled US$31.02&#160;million (2016 est.), consisting primarily of machinery and transportation equipment, foodstuffs, manufactured goods, fuels and lubricants.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_8-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Island_Sunset_(4568325551).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Montserrat_Island_Sunset_%284568325551%29.jpg/220px-Montserrat_Island_Sunset_%284568325551%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Montserrat_Island_Sunset_%284568325551%29.jpg/330px-Montserrat_Island_Sunset_%284568325551%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Montserrat_Island_Sunset_%284568325551%29.jpg/440px-Montserrat_Island_Sunset_%284568325551%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1739" data-file-height="1174" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Island_Sunset_(4568325551).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Montserrat from the <a href="/wiki/Guadeloupe_Passage" title="Guadeloupe Passage">Guadeloupe Passage</a></div></div></div>
<p>In 1979, <a href="/wiki/The_Beatles" title="The Beatles">The Beatles</a> producer <a href="/wiki/George_Martin" title="George Martin">George Martin</a> opened <a href="/wiki/AIR_Studios" class="mw-redirect" title="AIR Studios">AIR Studios Montserrat</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> making the island popular with musicians who often went there to record while taking advantage of the island's climate and beautiful surroundings.<sup id="cite_ref-AirMonserrat_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AirMonserrat-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> In the early hours of 17 September 1989, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Hugo" title="Hurricane Hugo">Hurricane Hugo</a> passed the island as a Category&#160;4 hurricane, damaging more than 90% of the structures on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_21-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> AIR Studios Montserrat closed, and the tourist economy was virtually wiped out.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> The slowly recovering tourist industry was again wiped out with the eruption of the Soufrière Hills Volcano in 1995, although it began partially to recover within fifteen years.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Airport_(15632994595).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Montserrat_Airport_%2815632994595%29.jpg/220px-Montserrat_Airport_%2815632994595%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Montserrat_Airport_%2815632994595%29.jpg/330px-Montserrat_Airport_%2815632994595%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Montserrat_Airport_%2815632994595%29.jpg/440px-Montserrat_Airport_%2815632994595%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Airport_(15632994595).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>John A. Osborne Airport</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Air">Air</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Air">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/John_A._Osborne_Airport" title="John A. Osborne Airport">John A. Osborne Airport</a> is the only airport on the island (constructed after the <a href="/wiki/W._H._Bramble_Airport" title="W. H. Bramble Airport">W. H. Bramble Airport</a> was destroyed in 1997 by the volcanic eruption). Scheduled service to <a href="/wiki/V._C._Bird_International_Airport" title="V. C. Bird International Airport">Antigua</a> is provided by FlyMontserrat<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/ABM_Air" class="mw-redirect" title="ABM Air">ABM Air</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Charter flights are also available to the surrounding islands.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sea">Sea</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Sea">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Ferry service to the island was provided by the Jaden Sun Ferry. It ran from Heritage Quay in <a href="/wiki/St._John%27s,_Antigua_and_Barbuda" title="St. John&#39;s, Antigua and Barbuda">St. John's, Antigua and Barbuda</a> to <a href="/wiki/Little_Bay,_Montserrat" title="Little Bay, Montserrat">Little Bay</a> on Montserrat. The ride was about an hour and a half and operated five days a week.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This service stopped in 2019 due to being financially unsustainable and the only access to Montserrat now is by air.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Montserrat" title="Demographics of Montserrat">Demographics of Montserrat</a></div>
<p>Montserrat had a population of 7,119 in 1842.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The island had a population of 5,879 (according to a 2008 estimate). An estimated 8,000 refugees left the island (<a href="/wiki/Montserratians_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Montserratians in the United Kingdom">primarily to the UK</a>) following the resumption of volcanic activity in July 1995; the population was 13,000 in 1994. The 2011 Montserrat census indicated a population of 4,922.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> In early 2016, the estimated population had reached nearly 5,000 primarily due to immigration from other islands.<sup id="cite_ref-2016Pop_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2016Pop-12">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Age structure (2003 estimates):
</p>
<ul><li>up to 14 years: 23.4% (male 1,062; female 1,041)</li>
<li>15 to 64 years: 65.3% (male 2,805; female 3,066)</li>
<li>65 years and over: 11.3% (male 537; female 484)</li></ul>
<p>The median age of the population was 28.1 as of 2002 and the sex ratio was 0.96 males/female as of 2000.
</p><p>The population growth rate is 6.9% (2008 est.), with a birth rate of 17.57 births/1,000 population, death rate of 7.34 deaths/1,000 population (2003 est.), and net migration rate of 195.35/1,000 population (2000 est.) There is an infant mortality rate of 7.77 deaths/1000 live births (2003 est.). The life expectancy at birth is 78.36 years: 76.24 for males and 80.59 for females (2003 est.). The total fertility rate is 1.8 children born/woman (2003 est.).
</p><p>According to a <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> estimate, the population as of April 2018 was 5,197 (for a density of 52 per square kilometre or 135 people per square mile), with just over 90% living in non-urban areas.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Language">Language</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Language">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a> is the sole official language and the main spoken language. A few thousand people speak <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_Creole" title="Montserrat Creole">Montserrat Creole</a>, a dialect of <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Caribbean_Creole_English" class="mw-redirect" title="Leeward Caribbean Creole English">Leeward Caribbean Creole English</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Historically <a href="/wiki/Irish_language" title="Irish language">Irish</a> was spoken, but not any more.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Irish_language_in_Montserrat">Irish language in Montserrat</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Irish language in Montserrat">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The Irish constituted the largest proportion of the white population from the founding of the colony in 1628. Most were <a href="/wiki/Indentured_servant" class="mw-redirect" title="Indentured servant">indentured servants</a>; others were merchants or plantation owners. The geographer Thomas Jeffrey claimed in <i>The West India Atlas</i> (1780) that the majority of those on Montserrat were either Irish or of Irish descent, "so that the use of the <a href="/wiki/Irish_language" title="Irish language">Irish language</a> is preserved on the island, even among the Negroes."<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>African slaves and Irish indentured servants of all classes were in constant contact, with sexual relationships being common and a population of mixed descent appearing as a consequence.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The Irish were also prominent in Caribbean commerce, with their merchants importing Irish goods such as beef, pork, butter and herring, and also importing slaves.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There is indirect evidence that the use of the Irish language continued in Montserrat until at least the middle of the nineteenth century. The <a href="/wiki/County_Kilkenny" title="County Kilkenny">Kilkenny</a> diarist and Irish scholar <a href="/wiki/Amhlaoibh_%C3%93_S%C3%BAilleabh%C3%A1in" title="Amhlaoibh Ó Súilleabháin">Amhlaoibh Ó Súilleabháin</a> noted in 1831 that he had heard that Irish was still spoken in Montserrat by both black and white inhabitants.<sup id="cite_ref-De_Bhaldraithe_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-De_Bhaldraithe-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1852, Henry H. Breen wrote in <i>Notes and Queries: a Medium of Intercommunication for Literary Men, etc.</i>, "The statement that 'the Irish language is spoken in the West India Islands, and that in some of them it may be said to be almost vernacular,' is true of the little Island of Montserrat, but has no foundation with respect to the other colonies."<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1902, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Irish_Times" title="The Irish Times">The Irish Times</a></i> quoted the <i>Montreal Family Herald</i> in a description of Montserrat, noting that "the negroes to this day speak the old Irish Gaelic tongue, or English with an Irish brogue. A story is told of a <a href="/wiki/Connaught" class="mw-redirect" title="Connaught">Connaught</a> man who, on arriving at the island, was, to his astonishment, hailed in a vernacular Irish by the black people."<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A letter by <a href="/wiki/William_Butler_(British_Army_officer)" title="William Butler (British Army officer)">W. F. Butler</a> in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Athenaeum_(British_magazine)" title="The Athenaeum (British magazine)">The Atheneum</a></i> (15 July 1905) quotes an account by a <a href="/wiki/Cork_(city)" title="Cork (city)">Cork</a> civil servant, C. Cremen, of what he had heard from a retired sailor called John O'Donovan, a fluent Irish speaker:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>"He frequently told me that in the year 1852, when mate of the brig Kaloolah, he went ashore on the island of Montserrat which was then out of the usual track of shipping. He said he was much surprised to hear the negroes actually talking Irish among themselves, and that he joined in the conversation..."<sup id="cite_ref-De_Bhaldraithe_93-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-De_Bhaldraithe-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>The British <a href="/wiki/Phonetics" title="Phonetics">phonetician</a> <a href="/wiki/John_C._Wells" title="John C. Wells">John C. Wells</a> conducted research into speech in Montserrat in 1977&#8211;78 (which included also Montserratians resident in London).<sup id="cite_ref-Wells_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wells-96">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> He found media claims that Irish speech, whether <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Irish" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglo-Irish">Anglo-Irish</a> or <a href="/wiki/Irish_Gaelic" class="mw-redirect" title="Irish Gaelic">Irish Gaelic</a>, influenced contemporary Montserratian speech were largely exaggerated.<sup id="cite_ref-Wells_96-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wells-96">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> He found little in phonology, morphology or syntax that could be attributed to Irish influence, and in Wells' report, only a small number of Irish words in use, one example being <i>minseach</i> <span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="und-Latn-fonipa">[ˈmʲiɲʃəx]</span> which he suggests is the noun <i>goat</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Wells_96-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wells-96">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2001, the CIA estimated the primary religion as Protestant (67.1%, including <a href="/wiki/Anglicanism" title="Anglicanism">Anglican</a> 21.8%, Methodist 17%, Pentecostal 14.1%, Seventh-day Adventist 10.5%, and Church of God 3.7%), with Catholics constituting 11.6%, Rastafarian 1.4%, other 6.5%, none 2.6%, unspecified 10.8%.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_8-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_groups">Ethnic groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Ethnic groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Residents of Montserrat are known as Montserratians. The population is predominantly, but not exclusively, of mixed African-Irish descent.<sup id="cite_ref-freepages.genealogy.rootsweb.ancestry.com_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-freepages.genealogy.rootsweb.ancestry.com-97">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> It is not known with certainty how many African slaves and indentured Irish labourers were brought to the West Indies, though according to one estimate some 60,000 Irish were "Barbadosed" by <a href="/wiki/Oliver_Cromwell" title="Oliver Cromwell">Oliver Cromwell</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-yale.edu_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-yale.edu-98">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> some of whom would have arrived in Montserrat.
</p><p>Data published by the <a href="/wiki/Central_Intelligence_Agency" title="Central Intelligence Agency">Central Intelligence Agency</a> indicates the ethnic group mix as follows (2011 est.):<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_8-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<dl><dd>88.4%: African/black</dd>
<dd><span style="visibility:hidden;color:transparent;">0</span>3.7%: mixed</dd>
<dd><span style="visibility:hidden;color:transparent;">0</span>3.0%: Hispanic/Spanish (of any race, including white)</dd>
<dd><span style="visibility:hidden;color:transparent;">0</span>2.7%: non-Hispanic Caucasian/white</dd>
<dd><span style="visibility:hidden;color:transparent;">0</span>1.5%: <a href="/wiki/Indo-Caribbean" class="mw-redirect" title="Indo-Caribbean">East Indian/Indian</a></dd>
<dd><span style="visibility:hidden;color:transparent;">0</span>0.7%: other</dd></dl>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Montserrat" title="Education in Montserrat">Education in Montserrat</a></div>
<p>Education in Montserrat is compulsory for children between the ages of 5 and 14, and free up to the age of 17. The only <a href="/wiki/Secondary_school" title="Secondary school">secondary school</a> (pre-16 years of age) on the island is the <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_Secondary_School" title="Montserrat Secondary School">Montserrat Secondary School</a> (MSS) in <a href="/wiki/Salem,_Montserrat" title="Salem, Montserrat">Salem</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-99">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_Community_College" title="Montserrat Community College">Montserrat Community College</a> (MCC) is a <a href="/wiki/Community_college" title="Community college">community college</a> (post-16 and tertiary educational institution) in Salem.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/University_of_the_West_Indies" title="University of the West Indies">University of the West Indies</a> maintains its Montserrat Open Campus.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/University_of_Science,_Arts_and_Technology" title="University of Science, Arts and Technology">University of Science, Arts and Technology</a> is a private medical school in <a href="/wiki/Olveston,_Montserrat" title="Olveston, Montserrat">Olveston</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Cultural_Center_(5809290561).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Montserrat_Cultural_Center_%285809290561%29.jpg/220px-Montserrat_Cultural_Center_%285809290561%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Montserrat_Cultural_Center_%285809290561%29.jpg/330px-Montserrat_Cultural_Center_%285809290561%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Montserrat_Cultural_Center_%285809290561%29.jpg/440px-Montserrat_Cultural_Center_%285809290561%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Montserrat_Cultural_Center_(5809290561).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Montserrat Cultural Centre overlooking Little Bay</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Montserrat" title="Music of Montserrat">Music of Montserrat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cricket_in_the_West_Indies" title="Cricket in the West Indies">Cricket in the West Indies</a></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Essay-like plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-style ambox-essay-like" href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>For more than a decade, George Martin's AIR Montserrat studio played host to recording sessions by many well known rock musicians, including <a href="/wiki/Dire_Straits" title="Dire Straits">Dire Straits</a>, <a href="/wiki/The_Police" title="The Police">The Police</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rush_(band)" title="Rush (band)">Rush</a>, <a href="/wiki/Elton_John" title="Elton John">Elton John</a>, <a href="/wiki/Michael_Jackson" title="Michael Jackson">Michael Jackson</a> and <a href="/wiki/The_Rolling_Stones" title="The Rolling Stones">The Rolling Stones</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-AirMonserrat_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AirMonserrat-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> After the volcanic eruptions of 1995 through 1997, and until his death in 2016, George Martin raised funds to help the victims and families on the island. The first event was a star-studded event at London's Royal Albert Hall in September 1997 (Music for Montserrat) featuring many artists who had previously recorded on the island including Paul McCartney, <a href="/wiki/Mark_Knopfler" title="Mark Knopfler">Mark Knopfler</a>, Elton John, <a href="/wiki/Sting_(musician)" title="Sting (musician)">Sting</a>, <a href="/wiki/Phil_Collins" title="Phil Collins">Phil Collins</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eric_Clapton" title="Eric Clapton">Eric Clapton</a> and <a href="/wiki/Midge_Ure" title="Midge Ure">Midge Ure</a>. The event raised £1.5&#160;million.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> All the proceeds from the show went towards short-term relief for the islanders.<sup id="cite_ref-AirMonserrat_78-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AirMonserrat-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Martin's second major initiative was to release five hundred limited edition lithographs of his score for the Beatles song "<a href="/wiki/Yesterday_(Beatles_song)" title="Yesterday (Beatles song)">Yesterday</a>". Complete with mistakes and tea stains, the lithographs are numbered and signed by <a href="/wiki/Paul_McCartney" title="Paul McCartney">Paul McCartney</a> and Martin. The lithograph sale raised more than US$1.4&#160;million which helped fund the building of a new cultural and community centre for Montserrat and provided a much needed focal point to help the re-generation of the island.<sup id="cite_ref-AirMonserrat_78-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AirMonserrat-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Many albums of note were recorded at <a href="/wiki/Associated_Independent_Recording" title="Associated Independent Recording">AIR Studios</a>, including Rush's <i><a href="/wiki/Power_Windows_(album)" title="Power Windows (album)">Power Windows</a></i>, Dire Straits' <i><a href="/wiki/Brothers_in_Arms_(album)" title="Brothers in Arms (album)">Brothers in Arms</a></i>; <a href="/wiki/Duran_Duran" title="Duran Duran">Duran Duran</a>'s <i><a href="/wiki/Seven_and_the_Ragged_Tiger" title="Seven and the Ragged Tiger">Seven and the Ragged Tiger</a></i>, The Police's <i><a href="/wiki/Synchronicity_(The_Police_album)" title="Synchronicity (The Police album)">Synchronicity</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Ghost_in_the_Machine_(The_Police_album)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ghost in the Machine (The Police album)">Ghost in the Machine</a></i> (videos for "<a href="/wiki/Every_Little_Thing_She_Does_Is_Magic" title="Every Little Thing She Does Is Magic">Every Little Thing She Does Is Magic</a>" and "<a href="/wiki/Spirits_in_the_Material_World" title="Spirits in the Material World">Spirits in the Material World</a>" were filmed in Montserrat), and <a href="/wiki/Jimmy_Buffett" title="Jimmy Buffett">Jimmy Buffett</a>'s <i>Volcano</i> (named for Soufrière Hills).<sup id="cite_ref-AirMonserrat_78-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AirMonserrat-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Ian_Anderson" title="Ian Anderson">Ian Anderson</a> (of <a href="/wiki/Jethro_Tull_(band)" title="Jethro Tull (band)">Jethro Tull</a>) recorded the song "Montserrat" on <i><a href="/wiki/The_Secret_Language_of_Birds" title="The Secret Language of Birds">The Secret Language of Birds</a></i> in tribute to the volcanic difficulties and feeling among residents of being abandoned by the UK government.
</p><p>In 2017, Montserrat was used to film much of the 2020 film <i><a href="/wiki/Wendy_(film)" title="Wendy (film)">Wendy</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Montserrat has one national radio station, <a href="/wiki/Radio_Montserrat" title="Radio Montserrat">Radio Montserrat</a>. The station offers a wide selection of music and news within the island and also on the internet for Montserratians living overseas.
</p><p>Notable shows include the <i>Morning Show</i> with Basil Chambers and Rose Willock's <i>Cultural Show</i>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cuisine_of_Montserrat" title="Cuisine of Montserrat">Cuisine of Montserrat</a></div>
<p>Montserrat's national dish is <a href="/wiki/Goat_water" title="Goat water">goat water</a>, a thick goat meat stew served with crusty bread rolls.<sup id="cite_ref-2016Pop_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2016Pop-12">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Montserrat cuisine resembles the general British and Caribbean cuisines, as it is situated in the Caribbean zone and it is a British territory. The cuisine includes a wide range of light meats, like fish, seafood and chicken, which are mostly grilled or roasted. Being a fusion of numerous cultures, such as Spanish, French, African, Indian and Amerindian, the Caribbean cuisine is unique and complex. More sophisticated meals include the Montserrat jerk shrimp, with rum, cinnamon bananas and cranberry. In other more rural areas, people prefer to eat homemade food, like the traditional mahi mahi and local breads.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Yachting">Yachting</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Yachting">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Montserrat is home to the Montserrat Yachting Association.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Athletics">Athletics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Athletics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Montserrat has competed in every <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_Games" title="Commonwealth Games">Commonwealth Games</a> since 1994.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Miguel_Francis" title="Miguel Francis">Miguel Francis</a> who now represents the United Kingdom and previously represented Antigua and Barbuda was born in Montserrat. He holds the Antiguan National record over <a href="/wiki/200m" class="mw-redirect" title="200m">200m</a> in 19.88.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Basketball">Basketball</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Basketball">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Basketball is growing in popularity in Montserrat with the country now setting up their own basketball league.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> The league contains six teams, which are the Look-Out Shooters, Davy Hill Ras Valley, Cudjoe Head Renegades, St. Peters Hilltop, Salem Jammers and MSS School Warriors.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> They have also built a new 800 seater complex which cost $1.5&#160;million.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cricket">Cricket</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Cricket">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In common with many Caribbean islands, <a href="/wiki/Cricket" title="Cricket">cricket</a> is a very popular sport in Montserrat. Players from Montserrat are eligible to play for the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies_cricket_team" title="West Indies cricket team">West Indies cricket team</a>. <a href="/wiki/Jim_Allen_(cricketer)" title="Jim Allen (cricketer)">Jim Allen</a> was the first to play for the West Indies and he represented the <a href="/wiki/World_Series_Cricket" title="World Series Cricket">World Series Cricket</a> West Indians, although, with a very small population, no other player from Montserrat had gone on to represent the West Indies until <a href="/wiki/Lionel_Baker" title="Lionel Baker">Lionel Baker</a> made his One Day International debut against Pakistan in November 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_cricket_team" title="Montserrat cricket team">Montserrat cricket team</a> forms a part of the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands_cricket_team" title="Leeward Islands cricket team">Leeward Islands cricket team</a> in regional domestic cricket, however it plays as a separate entity in minor regional matches,<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> as well having previously played <a href="/wiki/Twenty20" title="Twenty20">Twenty20</a> cricket in the <a href="/wiki/Stanford_20/20" title="Stanford 20/20">Stanford 20/20</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Two grounds on the island have held <a href="/wiki/First-class_cricket" title="First-class cricket">first-class</a> matches for the Leeward Islands, the first and most historic was <a href="/wiki/Sturge_Park" title="Sturge Park">Sturge Park</a> in Plymouth, which had been in use since the 1920s. This was destroyed in 1997 by the volcanic eruption. A new ground, the <a href="/wiki/Salem_Oval" title="Salem Oval">Salem Oval</a>, was constructed and opened in 2000. This has also held first-class cricket. A second ground has been constructed at Little Bay.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Football">Football</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Football">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Football_in_Montserrat" title="Football in Montserrat">Football in Montserrat</a>, <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_Football_Association" title="Montserrat Football Association">Montserrat Football Association</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_national_football_team" title="Montserrat national football team">Montserrat national football team</a></div>
<p>Montserrat has its own <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a> affiliated <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_national_football_team" title="Montserrat national football team">football team</a>, and has competed in the World Cup qualifiers five times but failed to advance to the finals from 2002 to 2018. A field for the team was built near the airport by FIFA. In 2002, the team competed in a friendly match with the second-lowest-ranked team in FIFA at that time, <a href="/wiki/Bhutan_national_football_team" title="Bhutan national football team">Bhutan</a>, in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Other_Final" title="The Other Final">The Other Final</a></i>, the same day as the final of the <a href="/wiki/2002_World_Cup" class="mw-redirect" title="2002 World Cup">2002 World Cup</a>. Bhutan won 4–0. Montserrat has failed to qualify for any <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">FIFA World Cup</a>. They have also failed to ever qualify for the <a href="/wiki/CONCACAF_Gold_Cup" title="CONCACAF Gold Cup">Gold Cup</a> and <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Cup" title="Caribbean Cup">Caribbean Cup</a>. The current national team relies mostly on the <a href="/wiki/Diaspora" title="Diaspora">diaspora</a> resident in <a href="/wiki/England" title="England">England</a> and in the last World Cup qualification game against <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_national_football_team" title="Curaçao national football team">Curaçao</a> nearly all the squad members played and lived in England.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Montserrat has a club league, the <a href="/wiki/Montserrat_Championship" title="Montserrat Championship">Montserrat Championship</a>, which has played sporadically since 1974. The league was most recently on hiatus from 2005 until 2015 but restarted play in 2016.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Surfing_Montserrat.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Montserrat&#39;s surfers, Carrll and Gary Robilotta" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b7/Surfing_Montserrat.jpg/170px-Surfing_Montserrat.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="206" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/b7/Surfing_Montserrat.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="225" data-file-height="272" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Surfing_Montserrat.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Surfer brothers Carrll and Gary Robilotta at Isle's Bay, Montserrat</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Surfing">Surfing</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Surfing">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Carrll Robilotta, whose parents moved from the United States to Montserrat in 1980, was responsible for pioneering the sport of surfing on the island. He and his brother Gary explored, discovered, and named the surf spots on the island during the 80's and early 90's.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Settlements">Settlements</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Settlements">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Little_Bay,_Montserrat.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Little_Bay%2C_Montserrat.jpg/220px-Little_Bay%2C_Montserrat.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Little_Bay%2C_Montserrat.jpg/330px-Little_Bay%2C_Montserrat.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Little_Bay%2C_Montserrat.jpg/440px-Little_Bay%2C_Montserrat.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Little_Bay,_Montserrat.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Little Bay, the site of the new capital. The project was funded by the UK's <a href="/wiki/Department_for_International_Development" title="Department for International Development">Department for International Development</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>Settlements within the exclusion zone are no longer habitable. See also <a href="/wiki/List_of_settlements_abandoned_after_the_1997_Soufri%C3%A8re_Hills_eruption" class="mw-redirect" title="List of settlements abandoned after the 1997 Soufrière Hills eruption">List of settlements abandoned after the 1997 Soufrière Hills eruption</a>.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_Montserratians">Notable Montserratians</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Notable Montserratians">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Jim_Allen_(cricketer)" title="Jim Allen (cricketer)">Jim Allen</a>, former cricketer who represented the <a href="/wiki/World_Series_Cricket" title="World Series Cricket">World Series Cricket</a> West Indians</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jennette_Arnold" title="Jennette Arnold">Jennette Arnold</a>, the first Montserratian elected as a Member of the <a href="/wiki/London_Assembly" title="London Assembly">London Assembly</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Lionel_Baker" title="Lionel Baker">Lionel Baker</a>, the first Montserratian to represent the West Indies in international cricket</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Arrow_(musician)" title="Arrow (musician)">Alphonsus "Arrow" Cassell</a>, musician known for his <a href="/wiki/Soca_music" title="Soca music">soca</a> song "<a href="/wiki/Hot_Hot_Hot_(Arrow_song)" title="Hot Hot Hot (Arrow song)">Hot Hot Hot</a>"</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Margaret_Dyer-Howe" title="Margaret Dyer-Howe">Margaret Dyer-Howe</a>, Montserrat's second woman to be appointed a cabinet minister.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ettore_Ewen" class="mw-redirect" title="Ettore Ewen">Ettore Ewen</a>, American professional wrestler and former WWE Heavyweight Champion, 11-time tag team champion, former college football player and powerlifter.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Howard_A._Fergus" class="mw-redirect" title="Howard A. Fergus">Howard A. Fergus</a>, author, poet and three time acting <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Montserrat#Governors_of_Montserrat,_1971–present" title="Governor of Montserrat">governor of Montserrat</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Patricia_Griffin" title="Patricia Griffin">Patricia Griffin</a>, pioneer nurse and volunteer social worker</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/George_Irish" title="George Irish">George Irish</a>, writer, human rights activist</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/E._A._Markham" title="E. A. Markham">E. A. Markham</a>, poet and author</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Dean_Mason" title="Dean Mason">Dean Mason</a>, association footballer</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Shane_Ryan_(social_activist)" title="Shane Ryan (social activist)">Shane Ryan</a>, writer, human rights activist</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Veronica_Ryan" title="Veronica Ryan">Veronica Ryan</a>, sculptor, and winner of the 2022 <a href="/wiki/Turner_Prize" title="Turner Prize">Turner Prize</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/M._P._Shiel" title="M. P. Shiel">M. P. Shiel</a>, writer</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Lyle_Taylor" title="Lyle Taylor">Lyle Taylor</a>, association footballer</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Rowan_Taylor_(footballer)" title="Rowan Taylor (footballer)">Rowan Taylor</a>, international footballer</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Maizie_Williams" title="Maizie Williams">Maizie Williams</a>, member of pop group <a href="/wiki/Boney_M" class="mw-redirect" title="Boney M">Boney M</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Angela_Yee" title="Angela Yee">Angela Yee</a>, member of the syndicated morning radio show <i><a href="/wiki/The_Breakfast_Club_(radio_show)" title="The Breakfast Club (radio show)">The Breakfast Club</a></i></li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montserrat&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">

<li id="cite_note-Plymouth-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Plymouth_1-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Ghost_town" title="Ghost town">Abandoned</a> in 1997, following a volcanic eruption, although it is still the <i>de jure</i> capital.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-Brades-2"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Brades_2-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Government buildings are now located in Brades, making it the <i>de facto</i> capital.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-4"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-4">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Minister of State in the <a href="/wiki/Foreign_and_Commonwealth_Office" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign and Commonwealth Office">Foreign and Commonwealth Office</a> with responsibility for the British Overseas Territories.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-118"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-118">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Includes the <a href="/wiki/John_A._Osborne_Airport" title="John A. Osborne Airport">new airport</a> in the north of the island.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<li id="cite_note-De_Bhaldraithe-93"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-De_Bhaldraithe_93-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-De_Bhaldraithe_93-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFDe_Bhaldraithe1979" class="citation book cs1 cs1-prop-foreign-lang-source-2"><a href="/wiki/Tom%C3%A1s_de_Bhaldraithe" title="Tomás de Bhaldraithe">De Bhaldraithe, Tomás</a>, ed. (1979). "Entry 2700, 1 Aibreán 1831 [1 April 1831]". <i><a href="/wiki/C%C3%ADn_Lae_Amhlaoibh" title="Cín Lae Amhlaoibh">Cín Lae Amhlaoibh</a></i> (in Ga). <a href="/wiki/Baile_%C3%81tha_Cliath" class="mw-redirect" title="Baile Átha Cliath">Baile Átha Cliath</a>: An Clóchomhar Tta. p.&#160;84. <q>Is clos dom gurb í an teanga Ghaeilge is teanga mháthartha i Monserrat san India Thiar ó aimsir Olibher Cromaill, noch do dhíbir cuid de chlanna Gael ó Éirinn gusan Oileán sin Montserrat. Labhartar an Ghaeilge ann go coiteann le daoine dubha agus bána. [I heard that the Irish language is the mother tongue in Montserrat in the West Indies since the time of Oliver Cromwell, who banished some Gaelic Irish families there. Irish speaking is common among both blacks and whites.]</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=bookitem&amp;rft.atitle=Entry+2700%2C+1+Aibre%C3%A1n+1831+%5B1+April+1831%5D&amp;rft.btitle=C%C3%ADn+Lae+Amhlaoibh&amp;rft.place=Baile+%C3%81tha+Cliath&amp;rft.pages=84&amp;rft.pub=An+Cl%C3%B3chomhar+Tta&amp;rft.date=1979&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AMontserrat" class="Z3988"></span></span>
